========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, yeah, nice to meet you. So, um, I guess what really made me finally call and ask for help is... well, I work retail, right? Over at the big store off the highway. And I'm on my feet all day, dealing with customers, stocking things, so usually I'd be starving by the middle of my shift. But lately, I just... I barely want to eat anymore. Like, at all. 

I'll be on my lunch break at the store, sitting in the back breakroom, and I'll have this food in front of me—stuff I used to really like, you know, like a big sandwich or whatever I grabbed on the way in. And I'll just sit there at the table staring at it. I'll just stare at it for my whole thirty-minute break. It just doesn't appeal to me whatsoever. I can't even force myself to take a bite most days, my stomach just sort of rejects the idea of it. So I just end up throwing most of it away in the trash can by the time clock before I have to clock back in and go back out to the sales floor. It's just a complete waste of money and food, but I just have zero appetite, and it's been happening every single day, which is why I figured I should probably come in and talk to somebody about it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I mean... down, I guess, but mostly it's just like... I don't really care about anything anymore. I used to have this whole routine, you know? Like, outside of the store. Because retail just completely drains you, so you gotta have things to look forward to. 

I used to get super pumped about going to these gallery nights downtown. They do this thing on the first Friday of every month where all the little local shops and art studios open their doors, and it's just a really cool vibe. I'd go with some buddies, and we'd always hit up the food trucks parked by the square—grabbing these amazing al pastor tacos from this one specific truck that's only ever there on weekends. Or, you know, playing pickup basketball at the rec center on Tuesday nights. I even used to volunteer sometimes, helping out the younger kids at the community center with their art stuff, like painting and drawing, since I've always kind of messed around with sketching and I liked seeing them get creative. 

But lately? Man, I just... I don't even know. My friends will try to drag me out to the courts, or we'll go to the gallery thing, and I just stand there. I'm just standing there on the sidewalk with a taco in my hand, or standing on the edge of the basketball court while everyone else is shooting around, and I just feel completely flat. Like, nothing. There's no spark, no excitement, no nothing. It's like my brain is just totally blank to it. So I usually just end up making some dumb excuse to leave early. I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. It's just exhausting trying to pretend I'm having fun when I'm just feeling so empty about all of it, you know? It's like the color got sucked out of everything I used to like doing.

Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. Like, it's not this constant dark cloud or anything like that where I'm just sitting around crying. It's more just... I don't know, like I was saying before, it's just this emptiness. But as for actually feeling down or depressed most of the day, for two weeks straight? No, I don't think it's been like that. 

I mean, I still go to work, right? I still clock in at the store. And when I'm on the floor, I'm just kind of going through the motions. I'll be stocking the shelves in the home goods section, making sure all the towels are folded right and the candles are faced forward, and I'm not really feeling sad while I'm doing it. I'm just... doing it. And then I'll have to deal with a customer who's upset about a return policy or something, and I just sort of nod and apologize and call a manager over, and I don't feel depressed then either. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. 

So it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.

I'm just standing there on the sidewalk with a taco in my hand, or standing on the edge of the basketball court while everyone else is shooting around, and I just feel completely flat. Like, nothing. There's no spark, no excitement, no nothing. It's like my brain is just totally blank to it. So I usually just end up making some dumb excuse to leave early. I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. It's just exhausting trying to pretend I'm having fun when I'm just feeling so empty about all of it, you know? It's like the color got sucked out of everything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. But as for actually feeling down or depressed most of the day, for two weeks straight? No, I don't think it's been like that. \n\nI mean, I still go to work, right? I still clock in at the store. And when I'm on the floor, I'm just kind of going through the motions. I'll be stocking the shelves in the home goods section, making sure all the towels are folded right and the candles are faced forward, and I'm not really feeling sad while I'm doing it. I'm just... doing it. And then I'll have to deal with a customer who's upset about a return policy or something, and I just sort of nod and apologize and call a manager over, and I don't feel depressed then either. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. \n\nSo it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that. I wouldn't say I've felt down or depressed most of the time on any particular day, honestly. It's just... it's hard to explain because I know people think "depression" and they think crying in bed all day, and I'm not doing that. 

Like yesterday, for example. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? There's this one guy who always has a coffee thermos that smells like hazelnut, and this older lady who knits. And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. 

And then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. So I'm hauling these heavy boxes of wicker chairs and glass tables off the pallets and onto the floor, sweating, my back is aching, and my manager is yelling about getting it done before the store opens at 8. And through all of that, I didn't feel depressed. I didn't feel anything. I was just moving the boxes because that's what I'm paid to do. 

Even when I got home around 3, I just sat on my couch. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. It's just this total absence of feeling. So, no, I really haven't had days where I felt down or depressed most of the time, not even mildly. It's just this constant flatline, you know? Like the battery is pulled out.

I barely want to eat anymore. Like, at all. \n\nI'll be on my lunch break at the store, sitting in the back breakroom, and I'll have this food in front of me—stuff I used to really like, you know, like a big sandwich or whatever I grabbed on the way in. And I'll just sit there at the table staring at it. I'll just stare at it for my whole thirty-minute break. It just doesn't appeal to me whatsoever. I can't even force myself to take a bite most days, my stomach just sort of rejects the idea of it. So I just end up throwing most of it away in the trash can by the time clock before I have to clock back in and go back out to the sales floor. It's just a complete waste of money and food, but I just have zero appetite, and it's been happening every single day, which is why I figured I should probably come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... down, I guess, but mostly it's just like... I don't really care about anything anymore. I used to have this whole routine, you know? Like, outside of the store. Because retail just completely drains you, so you gotta have things to look forward to. \n\nI used to get super pumped about going to these gallery nights downtown. They do this thing on the first Friday of every month where all the little local shops and art studios open their doors, and it's just a really cool vibe. I'd go with some buddies, and we'd always hit up the food trucks parked by the square—grabbing these amazing al pastor tacos from this one specific truck that's only ever there on weekends. Or, you know, playing pickup basketball at the rec center on Tuesday nights. I even used to volunteer sometimes, helping out the younger kids at the community center with their art stuff, like painting and drawing, since I've always kind of messed around with sketching and I liked seeing them get creative. \n\nBut lately? Man, I just... I don't even know. My friends will try to drag me out to the courts, or we'll go to the gallery thing, and I just stand there. I'm just standing there on the sidewalk with a taco in my hand, or standing on the edge of the basketball court while everyone else is shooting around, and I just feel completely flat. Like, nothing. There's no spark, no excitement, no nothing. It's like my brain is just totally blank to it. So I usually just end up making some dumb excuse to leave early. I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. It's just exhausting trying to pretend I'm having fun when I'm just feeling so empty about all of it, you know? It's like the color got sucked out of everything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. But as for actually feeling down or depressed most of the day, for two weeks straight? No, I don't think it's been like that. \n\nI mean, I still go to work, right? I still clock in at the store. I'll be stocking the shelves in the home goods section, making sure all the towels are folded right and the candles are faced forward, and I'm not really feeling sad while I'm doing it. I'm just... doing it. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. \n\nSo it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. So I'm hauling these heavy boxes of wicker chairs and glass tables off the pallets and onto the floor, sweating, my back is aching, and my manager is yelling about getting it done before the store opens at 8. And through all of that, I didn't feel depressed. I didn't feel anything. I was just moving the boxes because that's what I'm paid to do. \n\nEven when I got home around 3, I just sat on my couch. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. It's just this total absence of feeling. So, no, I really haven't had days where I felt down or depressed most of the time, not even mildly. It's just this constant flatline, you know? It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that. I wouldn't say I've felt down or depressed most of the time on any particular day, honestly. It's just... it's hard to explain because I know people think \"depression\" and they think crying in bed all day, and I'm not doing that. \n\nLike yesterday, for example. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? There's this one guy who always has a coffee thermos that smells like hazelnut, and this older lady who knits. And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely. I mean, the energy thing is just... it's huge right now. And it doesn't even seem to matter if I sleep, you know? I could be in bed for eight or nine hours, just laying there or sleeping or whatever, and I'll still wake up feeling like I just ran a marathon in my sleep. But work is where I really, really notice it. 

I mean, retail is always tiring anyway because you're on your feet all day on that cheap linoleum over concrete, and that messes with your knees and your back. But this is completely different. It's like... by the middle of a shift, my whole body just feels so incredibly heavy. Like my arms and legs are made out of wet cement or lead or something. 

I'll be out on the floor, right? And part of my job is just basic maintenance, making sure the store looks presentable for the regional managers or whoever. So I'll be over in the apparel section, and I have to straighten the clothing racks. Usually, you just breeze through it, you know? You just push the hangers back, space them out, make sure the sizes are in order, pick up the shirts people dropped on the floor. It's mindless. But lately, just lifting my arm to push a row of hangers feels like this massive, impossible chore. It takes way, way more out of me than it ever should. 

And going to the back? Man, forget about it. If a customer asks me to check if we have a different size in the back, I used to just jog back to the stockroom, check the rolling bins, and come right back out in two minutes. Now, just the thought of walking all the way across the store, pushing through those swinging double doors, and walking into the stockroom... it's exhausting just thinking about it. I have to physically drag myself back there. Every single step feels like I'm walking through deep mud. It's this deep, heavy exhaustion that just settles into my bones by like, noon, and I'll just find myself leaning against the endcaps or the register counter because just holding my own body upright takes too much effort.

additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before I started feeling this low after the award attention, I never believed coworkers were building a case against me or that my art was stolen. Those thoughts only showed up once I stopped enjoying things and started sleeping and eating badly.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’m still going to work, but I’ve been messing up inventory counts and display plans enough that my manager has had to double-check me. I’ve also been skipping gallery nights, basketball, and the youth art workshops I used to show up for.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since the window display award and all the attention from the district manager.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. Like, it's not this constant dark cloud or anything like that where I'm just sitting around crying. It's more just... I don't know, like I was saying before, it's just this emptiness. But as for actually feeling down or depressed most of the day, for two weeks straight? No, I don't think it's been like that. \n\nI mean, I still go to work, right? I still clock in at the store. And when I'm on the floor, I'm just kind of going through the motions. I'll be stocking the shelves in the home goods section, making sure all the towels are folded right and the candles are faced forward, and I'm not really feeling sad while I'm doing it. I'm just... doing it. And then I'll have to deal with a customer who's upset about a return policy or something, and I just sort of nod and apologize and call a manager over, and I don't feel depressed then either. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. \n\nSo it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that. I wouldn't say I've felt down or depressed most of the time on any particular day, honestly. It's just... it's hard to explain because I know people think \"depression\" and they think crying in bed all day, and I'm not doing that. \n\nLike yesterday, for example. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? There's this one guy who always has a coffee thermos that smells like hazelnut, and this older lady who knits. And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. So I'm hauling these heavy boxes of wicker chairs and glass tables off the pallets and onto the floor, sweating, my back is aching, and my manager is yelling about getting it done before the store opens at 8. And through all of that, I didn't feel depressed. I didn't feel anything. I was just moving the boxes because that's what I'm paid to do. \n\nEven when I got home around 3, I just sat on my couch. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. It's just this total absence of feeling. So, no, I really haven't had days where I felt down or depressed most of the time, not even mildly. It's just this constant flatline, you know? Like the battery is pulled out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a big drop in your energy level or feel more tired than usual, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, the energy thing is just... it's huge right now. And it doesn't even seem to matter if I sleep, you know? I could be in bed for eight or nine hours, just laying there or sleeping or whatever, and I'll still wake up feeling like I just ran a marathon in my sleep. But work is where I really, really notice it. \n\nI mean, retail is always tiring anyway because you're on your feet all day on that cheap linoleum over concrete, and that messes with your knees and your back. But this is completely different. It's like... by the middle of a shift, my whole body just feels so incredibly heavy. Like my arms and legs are made out of wet cement or lead or something. \n\nI'll be out on the floor, right? And part of my job is just basic maintenance, making sure the store looks presentable for the regional managers or whoever. So I'll be over in the apparel section, and I have to straighten the clothing racks. Usually, you just breeze through it, you know? You just push the hangers back, space them out, make sure the sizes are in order, pick up the shirts people dropped on the floor. It's mindless. But lately, just lifting my arm to push a row of hangers feels like this massive, impossible chore. It takes way, way more out of me than it ever should. \n\nAnd going to the back? Man, forget about it. If a customer asks me to check if we have a different size in the back, I used to just jog back to the stockroom, check the rolling bins, and come right back out in two minutes. Now, just the thought of walking all the way across the store, pushing through those swinging double doors, and walking into the stockroom... it's exhausting just thinking about it. I have to physically drag myself back there. Every single step feels like I'm walking through deep mud. It's this deep, heavy exhaustion that just settles into my bones by like, noon, and I'll just find myself leaning against the endcaps or the register counter because just holding my own body upright takes too much effort.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling like you’re worthless or felt guilty about things more than usual lately?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah... yeah, actually, I have. It's, um, it's been really messing with my head lately, especially at work. 

A couple of weeks ago, right around when things started feeling really off, my store manager pulled me into the back office. And usually, you know, when they call you back there, it's because somebody messed up a promotional display or a register was short at the end of the night, right? So I was already kind of bracing myself to get chewed out. But instead, she told me I was getting this "Team Member of the Quarter" award. They even gave me this little plastic pin for my lanyard and a twenty-dollar gift card to a steakhouse. And on top of that, she said the district manager was looking at me for an open shift supervisor position that's coming up next month. 

I know I should be happy about it, or at least care, but the thing is... I just feel sick to my stomach about the whole thing. I keep thinking I absolutely do not deserve that award. Like, at all. And I definitely don't deserve a chance at being a supervisor. I mean, I don't even have my high school diploma, and I can barely drag my own body to the stockroom right now, so how am I supposed to run a whole shift and tell other people what to do? 

And it's not just feeling worthless about it, it's this... this massive, crushing guilt. I feel like I've somehow tricked all of them. Like I've been running this huge scam, making them think I'm this great, reliable worker, and I'm actually just a total fraud. I even tried to talk to my manager about it the other day. I went up to her and said, you know, maybe they should give the supervisor spot to Marcus in electronics, because he's better with the customers and I don't think I'm right for it. But she just brushed it off. People keep telling me I earned it, that I'm a hard worker and I didn't trick anyone, but I just can't shake the feeling. I go in there every single day feeling like a criminal who's getting away with something, just waiting for everyone to figure out that I'm completely useless. It's just... it's exhausting carrying that around on top of how heavy my body already feels.

And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. Like my arms and legs are made out of wet cement or lead or something. \n\nI'll be out on the floor, right? I mean, I don't even have my high school diploma, and I can barely drag my own body to the stockroom right now, so how am I supposed to run a whole shift and tell other people what to do? \n\nAnd it's not just feeling worthless about it, it's this... this massive, crushing guilt. I feel like I've somehow tricked all of them. Like I've been running this huge scam, making them think I'm this great, reliable worker, and I'm actually just a total fraud. I even tried to talk to my manager about it the other day. I went up to her and said, you know, maybe they should give the supervisor spot to Marcus in electronics, because he's better with the customers and I don't think I'm right for it. But she just brushed it off. People keep telling me I earned it, that I'm a hard worker and I didn't trick anyone, but I just can't shake the feeling. I go in there every single day feeling like a criminal who's getting away with something, just waiting for everyone to figure out that I'm completely useless. It's just... it's exhausting carrying that around on top of how heavy my body already feels.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you completely lost interest or pleasure in absolutely everything you do, including activities you used to enjoy, even briefly?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"I used to get excited about gallery nights, playing basketball, grabbing food-truck tacos, or helping kids with art stuff, but lately I just stand there feeling flat and usually make an excuse to leave.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, that's... that's exactly what it is. It's like I was saying before about feeling blank at work, it just bleeds into everything else I do, too. Stuff I used to really look forward to, it just doesn't do anything for me anymore. Not even for a second. 

Like, I used to be huge into playing basketball. We had this regular pickup game at the park down the street, usually Tuesdays and Thursdays if the weather was good. I'd get my gear ready the night before, text the guys, get all hyped up to run some games. But I tried going a couple of weeks ago, and I was just standing there on the court while everyone else was running around, calling out plays and sweating. I just felt completely flat. I didn't care who had the ball, didn't care about the score. I just stood there for like ten minutes, faked a pulled hamstring, and made an excuse to leave. 

And it’s the same with going out. There’s this spot downtown where all the food trucks park on the weekends, and I used to get so excited to go down there and grab these amazing al pastor tacos with the pineapple on top. I’d crave them all week. My buddy dragged me down there recently, and I was just standing in line, looking at the trucks and smelling the food, and I just didn't care. I felt absolutely nothing. I ended up telling him I forgot I had to run an errand and just walked away to catch the bus before we even ordered. 

It even happens with the art stuff, which is what really bothers me. I used to volunteer sometimes at the community center, helping the neighborhood kids with their art stuff, setting up paints and markers and helping them draw. It was always really fun seeing what they’d come up with. And I’d go to these local gallery nights, too. You know, they have them on the first Friday of the month, where all the local artists show their work in these little pop-up spaces. I used to love going to those, just walking around for hours, getting inspired by what people were making. But the last time I went, I was just standing in this room full of bright paintings and people talking and laughing, and I just felt so incredibly flat and empty. It was like I was watching it all happen on a TV with the volume muted. I couldn't even force myself to pretend to be interested, so I just told the person I came with that I had a migraine and went home. It's just... everything is like that now. There's just zero spark for any of it.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n7\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.9, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3, F33.4, F33.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, yeah, nice to meet you. So, um, I guess what really made me finally call and ask for help is... well, I work retail, right? Over at the big store off the highway. And I'm on my feet all day, dealing with customers, stocking things, so usually I'd be starving by the middle of my shift. But lately, I just... I barely want to eat anymore. Like, at all. \n\nI'll be on my lunch break at the store, sitting in the back breakroom, and I'll have this food in front of me—stuff I used to really like, you know, like a big sandwich or whatever I grabbed on the way in. And I'll just sit there at the table staring at it. I'll just stare at it for my whole thirty-minute break. It just doesn't appeal to me whatsoever. I can't even force myself to take a bite most days, my stomach just sort of rejects the idea of it. So I just end up throwing most of it away in the trash can by the time clock before I have to clock back in and go back out to the sales floor. It's just a complete waste of money and food, but I just have zero appetite, and it's been happening every single day, which is why I figured I should probably come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... down, I guess, but mostly it's just like... I don't really care about anything anymore. I used to have this whole routine, you know? Like, outside of the store. Because retail just completely drains you, so you gotta have things to look forward to. \n\nI used to get super pumped about going to these gallery nights downtown. They do this thing on the first Friday of every month where all the little local shops and art studios open their doors, and it's just a really cool vibe. I'd go with some buddies, and we'd always hit up the food trucks parked by the square—grabbing these amazing al pastor tacos from this one specific truck that's only ever there on weekends. Or, you know, playing pickup basketball at the rec center on Tuesday nights. I even used to volunteer sometimes, helping out the younger kids at the community center with their art stuff, like painting and drawing, since I've always kind of messed around with sketching and I liked seeing them get creative. \n\nBut lately? Man, I just... I don't even know. My friends will try to drag me out to the courts, or we'll go to the gallery thing, and I just stand there. I'm just standing there on the sidewalk with a taco in my hand, or standing on the edge of the basketball court while everyone else is shooting around, and I just feel completely flat. Like, nothing. There's no spark, no excitement, no nothing. It's like my brain is just totally blank to it. So I usually just end up making some dumb excuse to leave early. I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. It's just exhausting trying to pretend I'm having fun when I'm just feeling so empty about all of it, you know? It's like the color got sucked out of everything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. Like, it's not this constant dark cloud or anything like that where I'm just sitting around crying. It's more just... I don't know, like I was saying before, it's just this emptiness. But as for actually feeling down or depressed most of the day, for two weeks straight? No, I don't think it's been like that. \n\nI mean, I still go to work, right? I still clock in at the store. And when I'm on the floor, I'm just kind of going through the motions. I'll be stocking the shelves in the home goods section, making sure all the towels are folded right and the candles are faced forward, and I'm not really feeling sad while I'm doing it. I'm just... doing it. And then I'll have to deal with a customer who's upset about a return policy or something, and I just sort of nod and apologize and call a manager over, and I don't feel depressed then either. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. \n\nSo it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that. I wouldn't say I've felt down or depressed most of the time on any particular day, honestly. It's just... it's hard to explain because I know people think \"depression\" and they think crying in bed all day, and I'm not doing that. \n\nLike yesterday, for example. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? There's this one guy who always has a coffee thermos that smells like hazelnut, and this older lady who knits. And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. So I'm hauling these heavy boxes of wicker chairs and glass tables off the pallets and onto the floor, sweating, my back is aching, and my manager is yelling about getting it done before the store opens at 8. And through all of that, I didn't feel depressed. I didn't feel anything. I was just moving the boxes because that's what I'm paid to do. \n\nEven when I got home around 3, I just sat on my couch. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. It's just this total absence of feeling. So, no, I really haven't had days where I felt down or depressed most of the time, not even mildly. It's just this constant flatline, you know? Like the battery is pulled out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a big drop in your energy level or feel more tired than usual, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, the energy thing is just... it's huge right now. And it doesn't even seem to matter if I sleep, you know? I could be in bed for eight or nine hours, just laying there or sleeping or whatever, and I'll still wake up feeling like I just ran a marathon in my sleep. But work is where I really, really notice it. \n\nI mean, retail is always tiring anyway because you're on your feet all day on that cheap linoleum over concrete, and that messes with your knees and your back. But this is completely different. It's like... by the middle of a shift, my whole body just feels so incredibly heavy. Like my arms and legs are made out of wet cement or lead or something. \n\nI'll be out on the floor, right? And part of my job is just basic maintenance, making sure the store looks presentable for the regional managers or whoever. So I'll be over in the apparel section, and I have to straighten the clothing racks. Usually, you just breeze through it, you know? You just push the hangers back, space them out, make sure the sizes are in order, pick up the shirts people dropped on the floor. It's mindless. But lately, just lifting my arm to push a row of hangers feels like this massive, impossible chore. It takes way, way more out of me than it ever should. \n\nAnd going to the back? Man, forget about it. If a customer asks me to check if we have a different size in the back, I used to just jog back to the stockroom, check the rolling bins, and come right back out in two minutes. Now, just the thought of walking all the way across the store, pushing through those swinging double doors, and walking into the stockroom... it's exhausting just thinking about it. I have to physically drag myself back there. Every single step feels like I'm walking through deep mud. It's this deep, heavy exhaustion that just settles into my bones by like, noon, and I'll just find myself leaning against the endcaps or the register counter because just holding my own body upright takes too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re worthless or felt guilty about things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, actually, I have. It's, um, it's been really messing with my head lately, especially at work. \n\nA couple of weeks ago, right around when things started feeling really off, my store manager pulled me into the back office. And usually, you know, when they call you back there, it's because somebody messed up a promotional display or a register was short at the end of the night, right? So I was already kind of bracing myself to get chewed out. But instead, she told me I was getting this \"Team Member of the Quarter\" award. They even gave me this little plastic pin for my lanyard and a twenty-dollar gift card to a steakhouse. And on top of that, she said the district manager was looking at me for an open shift supervisor position that's coming up next month. \n\nI know I should be happy about it, or at least care, but the thing is... I just feel sick to my stomach about the whole thing. I keep thinking I absolutely do not deserve that award. Like, at all. And I definitely don't deserve a chance at being a supervisor. I mean, I don't even have my high school diploma, and I can barely drag my own body to the stockroom right now, so how am I supposed to run a whole shift and tell other people what to do? \n\nAnd it's not just feeling worthless about it, it's this... this massive, crushing guilt. I feel like I've somehow tricked all of them. Like I've been running this huge scam, making them think I'm this great, reliable worker, and I'm actually just a total fraud. I even tried to talk to my manager about it the other day. I went up to her and said, you know, maybe they should give the supervisor spot to Marcus in electronics, because he's better with the customers and I don't think I'm right for it. But she just brushed it off. People keep telling me I earned it, that I'm a hard worker and I didn't trick anyone, but I just can't shake the feeling. I go in there every single day feeling like a criminal who's getting away with something, just waiting for everyone to figure out that I'm completely useless. It's just... it's exhausting carrying that around on top of how heavy my body already feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost interest or pleasure in absolutely everything you do, including activities you used to enjoy, even briefly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's exactly what it is. Not even for a second. \n\nLike, I used to be huge into playing basketball. We had this regular pickup game at the park down the street, usually Tuesdays and Thursdays if the weather was good. I'd get my gear ready the night before, text the guys, get all hyped up to run some games. But I tried going a couple of weeks ago, and I was just standing there on the court while everyone else was running around, calling out plays and sweating. I just felt completely flat. I didn't care who had the ball, didn't care about the score. I just stood there for like ten minutes, faked a pulled hamstring, and made an excuse to leave. \n\nAnd it’s the same with going out. No, not really. I know I used the word mud earlier when we were talking about how tired I am, but that’s more about how much effort everything takes, not that I’m actually moving in slow motion. 

Like, if you watched me work, I don't think I look any slower to anyone else. I was on the register just the other day—it was Tuesday, I think, because that’s when the new apparel shipment comes in and the store is always an absolute mess with cardboard boxes everywhere—and I was ringing people up just as fast as I always do. My hands were moving fine, scanning the barcodes, folding the shirts, bagging everything up, doing the whole customer service routine. I wasn't lagging behind the other cashiers or moving like a zombie in a movie or anything. I can still keep up with the line just fine. 

And my thoughts definitely aren't in slow motion, either. If anything, my brain is just constantly going, but it's all focused on that guilt stuff I was just telling you about. Like, I'll be standing there scanning a huge stack of clearance jeans, and my mind is just firing off all these reasons why I'm a fraud and why I shouldn't get that supervisor job, just rapid-fire, over and over. I can still remember all the PLU codes for the random items that are missing their price tags, and I can still do the math for the change in my head if the register computer freezes up, which it does all the time because our system is ancient. So my brain is definitely working at regular speed. 

It’s just that physically, my body feels incredibly heavy, but I'm still forcing myself to push through it at a normal pace. I'm not, like, stuck in slow-mo or taking ten minutes to process a thought or answer a question. I actually kind of wish my brain would slow down sometimes, honestly, just so I wouldn't have to think so much about the promotion and the award and all of that. But yeah, no, the actual speed of my thinking and moving hasn't really changed.

I can't even force myself to take a bite most days, my stomach just sort of rejects the idea of it. So I just end up throwing most of it away in the trash can by the time clock before I have to clock back in and go back out to the sales floor. It's just a complete waste of money and food, but I just have zero appetite, and it's been happening every single day, which is why I figured I should probably come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... down, I guess, but mostly it's just like... I don't really care about anything anymore. I used to have this whole routine, you know? Like, outside of the store. Because retail just completely drains you, so you gotta have things to look forward to. \n\nI used to get super pumped about going to these gallery nights downtown. They do this thing on the first Friday of every month where all the little local shops and art studios open their doors, and it's just a really cool vibe. I'd go with some buddies, and we'd always hit up the food trucks parked by the square—grabbing these amazing al pastor tacos from this one specific truck that's only ever there on weekends. Or, you know, playing pickup basketball at the rec center on Tuesday nights. I even used to volunteer sometimes, helping out the younger kids at the community center with their art stuff, like painting and drawing, since I've always kind of messed around with sketching and I liked seeing them get creative. \n\nBut lately? Man, I just... I don't even know. My friends will try to drag me out to the courts, or we'll go to the gallery thing, and I just stand there. I'm just standing there on the sidewalk with a taco in my hand, or standing on the edge of the basketball court while everyone else is shooting around, and I just feel completely flat. Like, nothing. There's no spark, no excitement, no nothing. It's like my brain is just totally blank to it. So I usually just end up making some dumb excuse to leave early. I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. It's just exhausting trying to pretend I'm having fun when I'm just feeling so empty about all of it, you know? It's like the color got sucked out of everything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. Like, it's not this constant dark cloud or anything like that where I'm just sitting around crying. It's more just... I don't know, like I was saying before, it's just this emptiness. But as for actually feeling down or depressed most of the day, for two weeks straight? No, I don't think it's been like that. \n\nI mean, I still go to work, right? I still clock in at the store. And when I'm on the floor, I'm just kind of going through the motions. I'll be stocking the shelves in the home goods section, making sure all the towels are folded right and the candles are faced forward, and I'm not really feeling sad while I'm doing it. I'm just... doing it. And then I'll have to deal with a customer who's upset about a return policy or something, and I just sort of nod and apologize and call a manager over, and I don't feel depressed then either. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. \n\nSo it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that. I wouldn't say I've felt down or depressed most of the time on any particular day, honestly. It's just... it's hard to explain because I know people think \"depression\" and they think crying in bed all day, and I'm not doing that. \n\nLike yesterday, for example. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? There's this one guy who always has a coffee thermos that smells like hazelnut, and this older lady who knits. And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. So I'm hauling these heavy boxes of wicker chairs and glass tables off the pallets and onto the floor, sweating, my back is aching, and my manager is yelling about getting it done before the store opens at 8. And through all of that, I didn't feel depressed. I didn't feel anything. I was just moving the boxes because that's what I'm paid to do. \n\nEven when I got home around 3, I just sat on my couch. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. It's just this total absence of feeling. So, no, I really haven't had days where I felt down or depressed most of the time, not even mildly. It's just this constant flatline, you know? Like the battery is pulled out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a big drop in your energy level or feel more tired than usual, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, the energy thing is just... it's huge right now. And it doesn't even seem to matter if I sleep, you know? I could be in bed for eight or nine hours, just laying there or sleeping or whatever, and I'll still wake up feeling like I just ran a marathon in my sleep. But work is where I really, really notice it. \n\nI mean, retail is always tiring anyway because you're on your feet all day on that cheap linoleum over concrete, and that messes with your knees and your back. But this is completely different. It's like... by the middle of a shift, my whole body just feels so incredibly heavy. Like my arms and legs are made out of wet cement or lead or something. \n\nI'll be out on the floor, right? And part of my job is just basic maintenance, making sure the store looks presentable for the regional managers or whoever. So I'll be over in the apparel section, and I have to straighten the clothing racks. Usually, you just breeze through it, you know? You just push the hangers back, space them out, make sure the sizes are in order, pick up the shirts people dropped on the floor. It's mindless. But lately, just lifting my arm to push a row of hangers feels like this massive, impossible chore. It takes way, way more out of me than it ever should. \n\nAnd going to the back? Man, forget about it. If a customer asks me to check if we have a different size in the back, I used to just jog back to the stockroom, check the rolling bins, and come right back out in two minutes. Now, just the thought of walking all the way across the store, pushing through those swinging double doors, and walking into the stockroom... it's exhausting just thinking about it. I have to physically drag myself back there. Every single step feels like I'm walking through deep mud. It's this deep, heavy exhaustion that just settles into my bones by like, noon, and I'll just find myself leaning against the endcaps or the register counter because just holding my own body upright takes too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re worthless or felt guilty about things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, actually, I have. It's, um, it's been really messing with my head lately, especially at work. \n\nA couple of weeks ago, right around when things started feeling really off, my store manager pulled me into the back office. And usually, you know, when they call you back there, it's because somebody messed up a promotional display or a register was short at the end of the night, right? So I was already kind of bracing myself to get chewed out. But instead, she told me I was getting this \"Team Member of the Quarter\" award. They even gave me this little plastic pin for my lanyard and a twenty-dollar gift card to a steakhouse. And on top of that, she said the district manager was looking at me for an open shift supervisor position that's coming up next month. \n\nI know I should be happy about it, or at least care, but the thing is... I just feel sick to my stomach about the whole thing. I keep thinking I absolutely do not deserve that award. Like, at all. And I definitely don't deserve a chance at being a supervisor. I mean, I don't even have my high school diploma, and I can barely drag my own body to the stockroom right now, so how am I supposed to run a whole shift and tell other people what to do? \n\nAnd it's not just feeling worthless about it, it's this... this massive, crushing guilt. I feel like I've somehow tricked all of them. Like I've been running this huge scam, making them think I'm this great, reliable worker, and I'm actually just a total fraud. I even tried to talk to my manager about it the other day. I went up to her and said, you know, maybe they should give the supervisor spot to Marcus in electronics, because he's better with the customers and I don't think I'm right for it. But she just brushed it off. People keep telling me I earned it, that I'm a hard worker and I didn't trick anyone, but I just can't shake the feeling. I go in there every single day feeling like a criminal who's getting away with something, just waiting for everyone to figure out that I'm completely useless. It's just... it's exhausting carrying that around on top of how heavy my body already feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost interest or pleasure in absolutely everything you do, including activities you used to enjoy, even briefly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's exactly what it is. It's like I was saying before about feeling blank at work, it just bleeds into everything else I do, too. Stuff I used to really look forward to, it just doesn't do anything for me anymore. Not even for a second. \n\nLike, I used to be huge into playing basketball. We had this regular pickup game at the park down the street, usually Tuesdays and Thursdays if the weather was good. I'd get my gear ready the night before, text the guys, get all hyped up to run some games. But I tried going a couple of weeks ago, and I was just standing there on the court while everyone else was running around, calling out plays and sweating. I just felt completely flat. I didn't care who had the ball, didn't care about the score. I just stood there for like ten minutes, faked a pulled hamstring, and made an excuse to leave. \n\nAnd it’s the same with going out. There’s this spot downtown where all the food trucks park on the weekends, and I used to get so excited to go down there and grab these amazing al pastor tacos with the pineapple on top. I’d crave them all week. My buddy dragged me down there recently, and I was just standing in line, looking at the trucks and smelling the food, and I just didn't care. I felt absolutely nothing. I ended up telling him I forgot I had to run an errand and just walked away to catch the bus before we even ordered. \n\nIt even happens with the art stuff, which is what really bothers me. I used to volunteer sometimes at the community center, helping the neighborhood kids with their art stuff, setting up paints and markers and helping them draw. It was always really fun seeing what they’d come up with. And I’d go to these local gallery nights, too. You know, they have them on the first Friday of the month, where all the local artists show their work in these little pop-up spaces. I used to love going to those, just walking around for hours, getting inspired by what people were making. But the last time I went, I was just standing in this room full of bright paintings and people talking and laughing, and I just felt so incredibly flat and empty. It was like I was watching it all happen on a TV with the volume muted. I couldn't even force myself to pretend to be interested, so I just told the person I came with that I had a migraine and went home. It's just... everything is like that now. There's just zero spark for any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or thinking more slowly than usual lately, like your thoughts are stuck in slow motion or your body feels like it’s moving through thick mud?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I know I used the word mud earlier when we were talking about how tired I am, but that’s more about how much effort everything takes, not that I’m actually moving in slow motion. \n\nLike, if you watched me work, I don't think I look any slower to anyone else. I was on the register just the other day—it was Tuesday, I think, because that’s when the new apparel shipment comes in and the store is always an absolute mess with cardboard boxes everywhere—and I was ringing people up just as fast as I always do. My hands were moving fine, scanning the barcodes, folding the shirts, bagging everything up, doing the whole customer service routine. I wasn't lagging behind the other cashiers or moving like a zombie in a movie or anything. I can still keep up with the line just fine. \n\nAnd my thoughts definitely aren't in slow motion, either. If anything, my brain is just constantly going, but it's all focused on that guilt stuff I was just telling you about. Like, I'll be standing there scanning a huge stack of clearance jeans, and my mind is just firing off all these reasons why I'm a fraud and why I shouldn't get that supervisor job, just rapid-fire, over and over. I can still remember all the PLU codes for the random items that are missing their price tags, and I can still do the math for the change in my head if the register computer freezes up, which it does all the time because our system is ancient. So my brain is definitely working at regular speed. \n\nIt’s just that physically, my body feels incredibly heavy, but I'm still forcing myself to push through it at a normal pace. I'm not, like, stuck in slow-mo or taking ten minutes to process a thought or answer a question. I actually kind of wish my brain would slow down sometimes, honestly, just so I wouldn't have to think so much about the promotion and the award and all of that. But yeah, no, the actual speed of my thinking and moving hasn't really changed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

```json
{"candidates": ["F32.2", "F32.3", "F33.2", "F33.3"], "note": "The patient ruled out psychomotor retardation, which is a key criterion for certain subtypes of depression (e.g., melancholic features), but the core symptoms of depressed mood (or anhedonia and low energy), guilt, and functional impairment remain consistent with major depressive episodes (F32.2, F32.3) or recurrent depression (F33.2, F33.3). No additional disorders were added or excluded this turn.", "is_final": false}
```

It's been... yeah, definitely way more than two weeks. It's been about two months now, actually. 

It all really started right around the time I got that window display award, which is what led to all that other stuff I was telling you about earlier. It was, I don't know, maybe eight or nine weeks ago? Corporate had sent down this massive new visual merchandising directive for the front windows for the season change. And usually, my store manager just has us slap up the standard promotional posters and dress the mannequins in whatever the featured denim is for the week, nothing crazy. But we were really short-staffed that Tuesday, and she just told me to handle the whole front-of-house setup by myself. 

So I spent, like, three hours dragging all these heavy props out of the basement, setting up this whole layered look with the fake autumn leaves they sent us and the new outerwear line. I didn't even think it was that special, honestly. I was just trying to get it done as fast as possible so I could finally go on my lunch break because I was starving. But then the district manager did a surprise walk-through the very next morning. And he made this huge, massive deal out of it. He was taking pictures of my window on his iPad, saying he was sending it to the regional team, and suddenly I'm getting all this intense attention from him. He pulled me aside and started asking me about my long-term career goals, which is when the whole supervisor track thing started. 

And ever since that exact week, about two months ago... that's when all of this just started crushing me. Because everyone is acting like I'm some kind of retail genius who put all this brilliant thought into a window display, and I literally just threw some fake leaves around some jackets because I wanted to go to the food court and get a pretzel. So yeah, it’s been two solid months of this. Two months of feeling like a complete fraud every single time I walk past that front window, just waiting for the district manager to realize he made a huge mistake. It feels like it's been a year, honestly, just dragging myself in there day after day since that visit...

I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. I'm just... doing it. It's just... it's hard to explain because I know people think \"depression\" and they think crying in bed all day, and I'm not doing that. \n\nLike yesterday, for example. I had the opening shift, so I was up at 4:30 AM, taking the bus in the dark. And usually, taking the early bus, you see the same people, right? There's this one guy who always has a coffee thermos that smells like hazelnut, and this older lady who knits. And I used to kind of people-watch and make up little stories in my head about where they were going. But yesterday, I just stared at the back of the seat in front of me. I wasn't sad about it. I wasn't feeling down. I was just... blank. \n\nAnd then I got to work, and we had to unload this massive truck of patio furniture because, you know, it's getting to be that season. So I'm hauling these heavy boxes of wicker chairs and glass tables off the pallets and onto the floor, sweating, my back is aching, and my manager is yelling about getting it done before the store opens at 8. And through all of that, I didn't feel depressed. I didn't feel anything. I was just moving the boxes because that's what I'm paid to do. \n\nEven when I got home around 3, I just sat on my couch. I didn't turn on the TV, didn't look at my phone. I just sat there until it got dark. But I wasn't sitting there feeling sad or down or miserable. I wasn't dwelling on anything bad. It's just this total absence of feeling. So, no, I really haven't had days where I felt down or depressed most of the time, not even mildly. It's just this constant flatline, you know? Like the battery is pulled out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a big drop in your energy level or feel more tired than usual, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, the energy thing is just... it's huge right now. And it doesn't even seem to matter if I sleep, you know? I could be in bed for eight or nine hours, just laying there or sleeping or whatever, and I'll still wake up feeling like I just ran a marathon in my sleep. But work is where I really, really notice it. \n\nI mean, retail is always tiring anyway because you're on your feet all day on that cheap linoleum over concrete, and that messes with your knees and your back. But this is completely different. It's like... by the middle of a shift, my whole body just feels so incredibly heavy. Like my arms and legs are made out of wet cement or lead or something. \n\nI'll be out on the floor, right? And part of my job is just basic maintenance, making sure the store looks presentable for the regional managers or whoever. So I'll be over in the apparel section, and I have to straighten the clothing racks. Usually, you just breeze through it, you know? You just push the hangers back, space them out, make sure the sizes are in order, pick up the shirts people dropped on the floor. It's mindless. But lately, just lifting my arm to push a row of hangers feels like this massive, impossible chore. It takes way, way more out of me than it ever should. \n\nAnd going to the back? Man, forget about it. If a customer asks me to check if we have a different size in the back, I used to just jog back to the stockroom, check the rolling bins, and come right back out in two minutes. Now, just the thought of walking all the way across the store, pushing through those swinging double doors, and walking into the stockroom... it's exhausting just thinking about it. I have to physically drag myself back there. Every single step feels like I'm walking through deep mud. It's this deep, heavy exhaustion that just settles into my bones by like, noon, and I'll just find myself leaning against the endcaps or the register counter because just holding my own body upright takes too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re worthless or felt guilty about things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, actually, I have. It's, um, it's been really messing with my head lately, especially at work. \n\nA couple of weeks ago, right around when things started feeling really off, my store manager pulled me into the back office. And usually, you know, when they call you back there, it's because somebody messed up a promotional display or a register was short at the end of the night, right? So I was already kind of bracing myself to get chewed out. But instead, she told me I was getting this \"Team Member of the Quarter\" award. They even gave me this little plastic pin for my lanyard and a twenty-dollar gift card to a steakhouse. And on top of that, she said the district manager was looking at me for an open shift supervisor position that's coming up next month. \n\nI know I should be happy about it, or at least care, but the thing is... I just feel sick to my stomach about the whole thing. I keep thinking I absolutely do not deserve that award. Like, at all. And I definitely don't deserve a chance at being a supervisor. I mean, I don't even have my high school diploma, and I can barely drag my own body to the stockroom right now, so how am I supposed to run a whole shift and tell other people what to do? \n\nAnd it's not just feeling worthless about it, it's this... this massive, crushing guilt. I feel like I've somehow tricked all of them. Like I've been running this huge scam, making them think I'm this great, reliable worker, and I'm actually just a total fraud. I even tried to talk to my manager about it the other day. I went up to her and said, you know, maybe they should give the supervisor spot to Marcus in electronics, because he's better with the customers and I don't think I'm right for it. But she just brushed it off. People keep telling me I earned it, that I'm a hard worker and I didn't trick anyone, but I just can't shake the feeling. I go in there every single day feeling like a criminal who's getting away with something, just waiting for everyone to figure out that I'm completely useless. It's just... it's exhausting carrying that around on top of how heavy my body already feels.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost interest or pleasure in absolutely everything you do, including activities you used to enjoy, even briefly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... that's exactly what it is. It's like I was saying before about feeling blank at work, it just bleeds into everything else I do, too. Stuff I used to really look forward to, it just doesn't do anything for me anymore. Not even for a second. \n\nLike, I used to be huge into playing basketball. We had this regular pickup game at the park down the street, usually Tuesdays and Thursdays if the weather was good. I'd get my gear ready the night before, text the guys, get all hyped up to run some games. But I tried going a couple of weeks ago, and I was just standing there on the court while everyone else was running around, calling out plays and sweating. I just felt completely flat. I didn't care who had the ball, didn't care about the score. I just stood there for like ten minutes, faked a pulled hamstring, and made an excuse to leave. \n\nAnd it’s the same with going out. There’s this spot downtown where all the food trucks park on the weekends, and I used to get so excited to go down there and grab these amazing al pastor tacos with the pineapple on top. I’d crave them all week. My buddy dragged me down there recently, and I was just standing in line, looking at the trucks and smelling the food, and I just didn't care. I felt absolutely nothing. I ended up telling him I forgot I had to run an errand and just walked away to catch the bus before we even ordered. \n\nIt even happens with the art stuff, which is what really bothers me. I used to volunteer sometimes at the community center, helping the neighborhood kids with their art stuff, setting up paints and markers and helping them draw. It was always really fun seeing what they’d come up with. And I’d go to these local gallery nights, too. You know, they have them on the first Friday of the month, where all the local artists show their work in these little pop-up spaces. I used to love going to those, just walking around for hours, getting inspired by what people were making. But the last time I went, I was just standing in this room full of bright paintings and people talking and laughing, and I just felt so incredibly flat and empty. It was like I was watching it all happen on a TV with the volume muted. I couldn't even force myself to pretend to be interested, so I just told the person I came with that I had a migraine and went home. It's just... everything is like that now. My hands were moving fine, scanning the barcodes, folding the shirts, bagging everything up, doing the whole customer service routine. I wasn't lagging behind the other cashiers or moving like a zombie in a movie or anything. I can still keep up with the line just fine. \n\nAnd my thoughts definitely aren't in slow motion, either. I can still remember all the PLU codes for the random items that are missing their price tags, and I can still do the math for the change in my head if the register computer freezes up, which it does all the time because our system is ancient. I'm not, like, stuck in slow-mo or taking ten minutes to process a thought or answer a question. I can't even force myself to take a bite most days, my stomach just sort of rejects the idea of it. So I just end up throwing most of it away in the trash can by the time clock before I have to clock back in and go back out to the sales floor. It's just a complete waste of money and food, but I just have zero appetite, and it's been happening every single day, which is why I figured I should probably come in and talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... down, I guess, but mostly it's just like... I don't really care about anything anymore. I used to have this whole routine, you know? Like, outside of the store. They do this thing on the first Friday of every month where all the little local shops and art studios open their doors, and it's just a really cool vibe. I'd go with some buddies, and we'd always hit up the food trucks parked by the square—grabbing these amazing al pastor tacos from this one specific truck that's only ever there on weekends. Or, you know, playing pickup basketball at the rec center on Tuesday nights. I even used to volunteer sometimes, helping out the younger kids at the community center with their art stuff, like painting and drawing, since I've always kind of messed around with sketching and I liked seeing them get creative. \n\nBut lately? Man, I just... I don't even know. My friends will try to drag me out to the courts, or we'll go to the gallery thing, and I just stand there. I'm just standing there on the sidewalk with a taco in my hand, or standing on the edge of the basketball court while everyone else is shooting around, and I just feel completely flat. Like, nothing. There's no spark, no excitement, no nothing. It's like my brain is just totally blank to it. So I usually just end up making some dumb excuse to leave early. I'll tell them I have an opening shift the next day and need to sleep, or that my stomach is acting up—which I guess isn't a total lie since I'm not eating anyway—but really I just want to get out of there. It's just exhausting trying to pretend I'm having fun when I'm just feeling so empty about all of it, you know? It's like the color got sucked out of everything I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I mean, I guess I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed all day, every day. I'm just... doing it. And then I'll have to deal with a customer who's upset about a return policy or something, and I just sort of nod and apologize and call a manager over, and I don't feel depressed then either. I just feel completely detached from the whole interaction. \n\nSo it's not like I'm walking around the store feeling miserable and depressed all day. It's just that when I have a second to think, or when I'm off the clock, that's when I notice that I just don't care about anything. But I wouldn't call it being depressed most of the day, every day. It's more just a lack of feeling anything at all, if that makes sense. I don't know if that's the same thing as being depressed, but it definitely doesn't feel like sadness. It just feels like... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the time, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was trying to explain, it's not really like that.
